# Record 20260804-164956-72883fb

- **Record ID**: 20260804-164956-72883fb
- **Claim**: #8 (design-input claim, and a pass/fail against two proposed spec lines) -- does the 5-stage current-starved ring VCO of `design/vco.sch` cover the ratified v1 output band of 10-200 MHz (DR-002 Decision 2) at every PVT corner, with adjacent-band overlap and no coverage hole, and with Kvco inside the ceiling DR-001 Decision 1's fixed loop filter assumes? Two of the checks below are binding on sibling issues: the **low-band floor** is DR-002 Decision 2's documented trigger for whether an output divider enters v1 scope (#11), and the **Kvco table** is the input #9/#10 size the charge pump and loop filter against. Spec-line references are placeholders pending ratification (#1): `spec/pll.md#output-band`, `spec/pll.md#kvco`.
- **Netlist provenance**: schematic export(s) `design/netlist/vco.spice` (SHA-256 `3d8bf1b307d7b230808c86b318262822fdf525c2503724609bdf51d1b1cc2d31`) composed with stimulus `sim/vco-tuning-range/testbench/tb_vco_tuning.spice` (SHA-256 `fab73ac36d339b9ae02c3f3172ecca46432179720a57fbd544e7e51a426b6892`); composed SHA-256 `75f6fbf7b344c738b29c3189e338740dd23c7ab47f5a40bafa1e68674202ffd0` — this is what the netlist snapshot holds
- **Environment provenance**:
  - PDK: volare `gf180mcuD`, open_pdks `c6d73a35f524070e85faff4a6a9eef49553ebc2b` (/home/ubuntu/.volare/gf180mcuD, found via search_root:~/.volare)
  - Models: `libs.tech/ngspice/sm141064.ngspice`; `design.ngspice` included first
  - Simulator: ngspice-42 : Circuit level simulation program. Harness: sim/harness 0.1.0, python 3.12.3
  - Repo commit: `72883fb759c0ab3f25ebdb3da62afa37e831fd38` (clean tree)
  - Host: Linux-7.0.0-1009-aws-x86_64-with-glibc2.39 (ip-172-31-74-176)
- **Corner matrix run**:
  - Process (bundle -> `.lib` sections): `typical` -> typical res_typical moscap_typical mimcap_typical; `ff` -> ff res_typical moscap_typical mimcap_typical; `ss` -> ss res_typical moscap_typical mimcap_typical; `fs` -> fs res_typical moscap_typical mimcap_typical; `sf` -> sf res_typical moscap_typical mimcap_typical; `all-slow` -> ss res_ss moscap_ss mimcap_ss; `all-fast` -> ff res_ff moscap_ff mimcap_ff
  - Temperature: -40 °C, 27 °C, 125 °C
  - Supply: 2.97 V, 3.30 V, 3.63 V
  - 504 point grid (process x temperature x supply x band), 504 completed
  - Full PVT matrix per CLAUDE.md / sim/README.md (-40/27/125 °C, ±10% supply, the five MOS process bundles).
  - Extra swept variables (beyond process x temperature x supply):
    - `band` — 3-bit static band-select code (B2 B1 B0), all 8 codes: `band0`, `band1`, `band2`, `band3`, `band4`, `band5`, `band6`, `band7`
- **Methodology / criteria / limitations**:
  - **Measurement topology**: seven independent copies of the complete VCO (constant-gm bias generator + band-select mirror chain + V->I converter + 5-stage starved ring + output buffer + on-chip decoupling) are instantiated in one deck, one per control voltage, at a common PVT point and band code. Each copy has its own supply ammeter, so f(Vctrl) and I(Vctrl) at all seven control points come from a single transient. The copies share only the ideal supply and ground nodes.
  - **Measurement criterion**: frequency is measured at the **buffered output `CLK`**, not at an internal ring node -- `CLK` is what the divider (#11) and the closed-loop bench (#12) see, and measuring there also proves the output buffer squares the starved ring's slow internal edges into a rail-to-rail clock at the bottom of the band. The period `tp<n>` is taken over four whole cycles between the 1st and 5th rising half-supply crossing after `w_settle`, and `w_settle` is >= 4 estimated periods, so the bias generator's own start-up transient is excluded from every number. `f<n> = 4/tp<n>`.
  - **Kvco extraction**: `k1..k7` are the local dF/dVctrl by central difference on the 0.3 V control grid (one-sided at the two ends), per (corner, band); `kvco_min`/`kvco_max`/`kvco_mean`/`kvco_over_f_max`/`fine_ratio` reduce each (corner, band) curve. Reported per point, not as a single per-band slope, because the loop-dynamics design (#10) consumes the Kvco **spread**, not its average.
  - Simulator settings: `.tran <tmax> <tstop> 0 <tmax>` with the window scaled per (band, temperature, supply) in-deck -- `tmax` <= 1/(80 f_max), i.e. >= 80 timesteps per cycle of the fastest instance in the deck, so half-supply crossings are interpolated to well under 1% of a period. The frequency envelope that sizes the window is a least-squares fit against record `20260731-175947-0a12e6c`'s 3528 measured points with a 1.10 safety margin (see the fragment header); it never enters a result -- an envelope wrong enough to cost a rising edge makes the `.measure` fail and the harness reports that point as failed. `uic` is deliberately NOT used: the bias generator is solved to its DC operating point before t=0. `.ic` is applied to the ring nodes only, to break the metastable all-nodes-at-mid DC solution.
  - The control block ends with `quit`. Without it ngspice's batch mode runs the whole transient a **second** time to service the netlist-level `.measure` cards, which on this campaign's 504-point matrix is hours of duplicated wall clock for byte-identical numbers.
  - Every point is self-checking: all seven `.meas` frequencies are required measurements, so a point that lost a rising edge is reported as failed rather than silently dropping a control point. No value in the tables below is an extrapolation or a default.
  - Statistical switches: `sw_stat_global = sw_stat_mismatch = 0` (nominal per-corner skew, no Monte Carlo mismatch). Device mismatch in the band-select mirror is **not** covered by this record.
  - **Limitation -- schematic-level, no parasitics.** Ring frequency is set by the load capacitance of each stage, so layout parasitics will move every number here downward. The margins reported below are the budget that extraction (#18) has to stay inside, not a final result.
  - **Limitation -- clean supply.** This record sweeps DC supply only; it produces a supply-*pushing* number but no jitter number. DR-001 names supply noise the top risk for this topology, and the supply-step / supply-ripple jitter testbench that addresses it is a separate record from `testbench/run_supply.sh`. A tuning-range record on its own does not discharge that acceptance criterion.
  - **Limitation -- Vctrl endpoints, not a servo.** Bands are characterized over a fixed 0.9-2.7 V control window; the closed loop will not use the full window at every band. Band-edge numbers are therefore the *available* range.
  - **Passive axes ARE swept, and deliberately so.** This DUT's frequency is set by a poly resistor (the V->I degeneration resistor and the constant-gm reference resistor), so the resistor corner is the single most Kvco-relevant process axis. The five MOS-only bundles are kept for comparability with `sim/devchar-delay`; `all-slow`/`all-fast` skew every family together so every reported worst case is a genuine worst case. The MIM sections those two bundles also carry are inert here -- the decoupling capacitor is `cap_nmos_03v3` (a MOS cap), there is no MIM device in this DUT.
- **Statistical convention**: N/A (corner-matrix claim, not a distribution claim)
- **Result**:

  **acceptance_checks** (derived) — issue #8's acceptance checks, each against its spec line, with the corner that binds it named (spec lines: 10-200 MHz v1 output band per DR-002 Decision 2; Kvco ceiling per DR-001 Decision 1)

  **Overall: PASS.**
  
  The ring covers the ratified 10-200 MHz v1 output band at every one
  of the 63 corners on the grid -- reaching below 10 MHz at the
  fastest corner and above 200 MHz at the slowest -- with no coverage
  hole, monotonic control, and Kvco inside DR-001 Decision 1's ceiling
  at the band a correct configuration selects.
  
  **Caveat that must travel with these numbers (check 4b).** Kvco
  reaches 154.4 MHz/V at an in-band operating point in band B7, which
  is above DR-001's ~150 MHz/V bound. That point is only reachable by
  configuring a *higher* band than the target frequency requires. It
  is not a defect of the ring, but it does mean the band code is part
  of the loop-stability contract, not just a range-selection
  convenience -- #9/#10 must state the band-selection rule alongside
  the filter values, and a part configured into too high a band will
  present the loop with more gain than the fixed filter was sized for.
  
  **What this record hands to the sibling issues:**
  
    - **#11 (output divider), DR-002 Decision 2's conditional trigger**: the
      lowest band reaches 6.451 MHz at the fastest corner, 35 % below the
      10 MHz spec line, so the ring reaches the bottom of the band **without**
      a post-VCO divider. The DR-002 trigger does **not** fire: no output
      divider enters v1 scope on account of the VCO's low-band floor.
    - **#9 / #10 (charge pump, loop filter)**: size against the per-point Kvco
      columns (k1..k7, kvco_min/kvco_max) below, not against a single Kvco
      number. Within the v1 band Kvco spans 3.243 .. 154.4 MHz/V across bands and
      corners.
    - **#14 (supply sensitivity)**: static pushing table below; the transient
      numbers are in the supply record from `run_supply.sh`.

  | # | check | binding corner | measured | spec line | verdict |
  |---|---|---|---|---|---|
  | 1 | Lowest band (B0) reaches DOWN to 10 MHz at **every** corner | all-fast/125C/2.97V | 6.451 MHz floor | <= 10 MHz | **PASS** |
  | 2 | Highest band (B7) reaches UP to 200 MHz at **every** corner | all-slow/-40C/3.63V | 247.7 MHz ceiling | >= 200 MHz | **PASS** |
  | 3 | No band-overlap hole inside 10-200 MHz at any corner | ss/125C/3.63V | worst adjacent overlap ratio 1.268 | >= 1.000 | **PASS** |
  | 4a | Kvco under the fixed-filter ceiling at the band a correct configuration selects | all-fast/27C/2.97V, target 200 MHz -> B6 @ 1.54 V | 115.7 MHz/V | <= 150 MHz/V | **PASS** |
  | 4b | Kvco under the ceiling at *every* in-band operating point, including bands a correct configuration would not select | all-fast/27C/3.30V B7 @ 0.9 V | 154.4 MHz/V | <= 150 MHz/V | **FAIL** |
  | 5 | f(Vctrl) monotonic on every (corner, band) curve | n/a | 0 non-monotonic curves | 0 | **PASS** |

  **kvco_summary** (derived) — Kvco across the whole grid, per band. The per-(corner, band, Vctrl) detail is the k1..k7 columns of the per-point table below, and the per-(corner, band) reduction is its kvco_min / kvco_max / kvco_mean / kvco_over_f_max / fine_ratio columns

    - Worst-case Kvco **at an operating point inside the ratified 10-200 MHz
      band, over ALL band codes**: 154.4 MHz/V (all-fast/27C/3.30V, B7, Vctrl 0.9 V, f =
      194.5 MHz) -- **OVER** DR-001's ~150 MHz/V ceiling. Reaching that point
      requires configuring band B7 for a frequency band B6 already
      covers; see check 4a and the band-choice note below.
    - Worst-case Kvco anywhere on the grid **including the bands above the v1
      band**: 206 MHz/V (all-fast/125C/2.97V, B7, Vctrl 1.5 V, f = 396.8 MHz).
      DR-002 Decision 2 budgets v1 margin to 200 MHz, so that point sits
      ABOVE the v1 envelope and exceeds the 150 MHz/V bound; it is reported
      because the deferred 400 MHz stretch would operate there.
    - Kvco/f_out inside the v1 band spans **0.31 .. 0.84 per volt**, which
      **brackets** DR-001's 0.7/V design-intent hand calc rather than
      confirming it: the ratio is not a constant of the topology, it rises
      monotonically with band code (see the per-band table above). Reading
      0.7/V as a single number would under-predict Kvco in the top bands and
      over-predict it in the bottom ones. #9/#10 should size against the
      per-band table, not against the hand calc.
  
    - **Band choice is load-bearing for the Kvco ceiling.** Because Kvco
      scales with f_osc *within* a band, two different band codes that both
      reach the same output frequency do not present the same Kvco to the
      loop: the higher band reaches it near the bottom of its Vctrl range,
      where its Kvco is already large. Selecting the **lowest** band that
      reaches the target frequency is therefore not a preference but a
      requirement of DR-001 Decision 1's fixed loop filter, and it is what
      check 4a evaluates against check 4b's adversarial band choice.
